> Bottom line: Dillon is not failing at prompting. The current system converts too many signals into isolated documents, does not maintain shared client state, and does not learn from feedback. More prompting is compensating for a broken operating model.
> 
> ## Ranked findings
> 
> 1. **Stop automatic execution on every matched inbox item. Highest leverage. Verified.**
> 
>    - `DillonAgentOS-GmailBridge` runs hourly with `poll --execute --notify`.
>    - Today it created 10 jobs and 9 deliverables totaling about 8,416 words.
>    - Outputs included duplicate KJB thread briefs, two NKCDC meeting artifacts, a generic calendar-invite brief, and a Pitchfork newsletter falsely assigned to client `Bridge` because `bridge` matched “Bridgers.”
>    - Slack is not causing today’s churn: its 15-minute polls returned zero events. Today’s executed jobs have `gmail:*` event keys.
>    - Any client substring match at confidence `0.55` executes. The quality gate only checks that a Markdown file exceeds 200 bytes and contains “evidence/assumption” and “next action.”
> 
>    Fix: leave watchers as intake only. Require an explicit task marker, exact sender/domain/channel match, real action intent, and priority threshold before execution. Add thread-level semantic deduplication, calendar/newsletter/automated-message suppression, per-client cooldowns, and a daily usage budget. Batch candidate work into one control board.

>    Fix: one persistent `client-operations` controller. > Exact safe syntax:
> 
> ```powershell
> & 'C:\Users\dillo\.codex\credential-bridge\Invoke-CodexBitwardenBridge.ps1' `
>   -Operation ResolveItem `
>   -Locator 'bw://item/00000000-0000-0000-0000-000000000000'
> ```
> 
> A plain GUID is not accepted. It must be wrapped as `bw://item/<guid>`; otherwise the bridge returns `exact_item_locator_required` with exit code 38. `-ExpectedOrigin` is not needed for `ResolveItem`.
> 
> Optional safe preflight:
> 
> ```powershell
> & 'C:\Users\dillo\.codex\credential-bridge\Invoke-CodexBitwardenBridge.ps1' -Operation Status
> ```
> 
> Successful output is compressed JSON containing only:
> 
> - `bridge_version`
> - `cli_version`
> - `operation`
> - `state`
> - `credential_present`
> - `locator`
> - `item.item_id`
> - `item.item_type`
> - `item.revision_date`
> - `item.https_hosts`
> 
> It does not return item names, usernames, passwords, notes, tokens, or URI paths. `https_hosts` contains only sanitized HTTPS hostnames for login items (`item_type = 1`); secure notes and other item types return an empty array.
> 
> Prerequisites enforced by the bridge:
> 
> - Pinned, signed, hash-verified Bitwarden CLI 2026.6.0 exists at the configured path.
> - Windows crash-dump capture is not enabled for `bw.exe`, `pwsh.exe`, or `powershell.exe`.
> - Access Broker has exactly one `dillon-operations` → `bitwarden-password-manager` route with a `bw://account/...` identity and valid HTTPS endpoint.

> - Windows Credential Manager contains `Codex.Bitwarden.Bootstrap.v1`.
> - Vault unlock, identity revalidation, and sync succeed.
> - The exact item exists and its returned ID matches the requested GUID.
> - The bridge mutex is available and its temporary session can be securely relocked.
> 
> Implementation evidence: `Invoke-CodexBitwardenBridge.ps1` lines 13–22, 883–950, 1092–1193, and 1224–1434.

> ### Critical findings

> 1. **Provider operations are not bound to the client or exact child account.**

> 
>    However, neither validator nor launcher reads either config. Provider packets accept a free-form `account` and arbitrary arguments, and the executor forwards them unchanged: [schema](C:/Users/dillo/Documents/Codex/projects/client-operations/schemas/ad-provider-packet.schema.json:29), [launcher](C:/Users/dillo/Documents/Codex/projects/client-operations/scripts/Invoke-AdLaunch.ps1:23), [executor](C:/Users/dillo/Documents/Codex/projects/client-operations/scripts/providers/composio_execute.py:58).
> 
>    A request approved for one client could therefore target another child account, platform, budget, or customer ID. Immohrtal is correctly modeled as a login identity in JSON, but that distinction is currently documentation only.
> 
> 2. **Canonical authority and readiness can be replaced at invocation time.**
> 
>    `Invoke-AdLaunch.ps1` exposes arbitrary authority, readiness, runtime-root, adapter, and historical `AsOf` overrides: [parameters](C:/Users/dillo/Documents/Codex/projects/client-operations/scripts/Invoke-AdLaunch.ps1:2). The validator accepts those paths without requiring the canonical client authority or canonical readiness file: [authority loading](C:/Users/dillo/Documents/Codex/projects/client-operations/scripts/Test-AdLaunchRequest.ps1:53).
> 
>    Existing tests deliberately fabricate active authority and ready-provider files outside canonical paths and then pass them successfully: [test fixtures](C:/Users/dillo/Documents/Codex/projects/client-operations/scripts/Test-MarketingOS.ps1:175). Caller-controlled `AsOf` can also make expired authority appear current.
> 
> 3. **Validate and Readback can execute mutating tools without authority.**
> 
>    Authority is enforced only for `CreatePaused` and `Enable`: [gates](C:/Users/dillo/Documents/Codex/projects/client-operations/scripts/Invoke-AdLaunch.ps1:41). Any `GOOGLEADS_*` or `METAADS_*` slug is otherwise accepted. Meta validation has no validate-only enforcement,
